# Droog's Diary — Voice Notes, 2008–2017

**Slice E of the voice-note archive.**  
**Source:** `Dropbox/Voice Notes/diary/`  
**Work:** `diary` — its own shelf, per routing ruling #2  
**Recordings:** 302 unique | **Audio:** 39.6 h | **Words:** 83,758 | **Source files:** 414  
**Engine:** Whisper `small.en` (int8, Silero VAD)

---

## How to read this file

Chronological. One `###` heading per recording, with every source filename that holds
that recording. 414 files in the folder collapse to 302 distinct recordings, because
the same take is often stored two or three times over — as a `.wav` and an `.mp3`, or
under both a `DW_` and a `DZ_` prefix, or with a stray `-1234` suffix. Those are folded
into one entry here and the alternate filenames listed beside it, so nothing in the
archive is orphaned but nothing is read twice.

**Dates are mostly unreliable.** Only 23 recordings carry a date recoverable from the
filename. The rest fall back to Dropbox metadata, and the whole WMA batch shares one
2013-05-26 bulk-upload stamp regardless of when it was spoken — those are marked
_(file date)_ and should be read as an upper bound, not a date. `DS_20337` is filed as
2013 and contains live radio of the Palin–Biden debate, which actually places it on
2008-10-02; other long recordings could probably be dated the same way.

**Markers.** `[no speech]` — a silent recording, kept in place so its absence doesn't
read as data loss. `_[repeated ×N]_` — a stutter or transcription loop collapsed.
`gain-rescued` — too faint for the first pass, re-run with loudness normalisation.
`[?]` would mark an uncertain word; proper nouns were corrected against the project
glossary by script rather than by guesswork.

> Ok, it's the 31st of August, 2004. I'm just about an hour away from Spokane, driving from
> Seattle to the pad. Last long drive in a while, and I'm so looking forward. It feels so good
> to be driving toward Cape Mountain. I smile when I say her name. It's what an effect a woman
> has on me. And our love has really changed. This day transformed it from what it would have
> been. Mostly very sad, blaming Inka. But because of Kate, most of my thoughts are of her.
> And, uh, that's just pure joy. Just, you know, I can send a gold thread of love right out
> the center of my chest. The moon is coming up ahead. It clouds up, pulling a trailer. Kept,
> saved my ass again. Packed that thing full. Uh, left the, left the, uh, apartment there and
> a shambles.
>
> There's a lot of stuff to dispose of, bookshelves and tables and whatnot, and I'm tired. I
> didn't see the house during it today, which I hadn't really planned on, but given how sick I
> was and our recent kerfuffle over Inga's claim that I owe, that I will pay and pay for all
> of Margaret's education. Yeah, a little easier on. So, anyway, tonight, maybe curdling
> and... Oh, who knows? Bismarck. And so, under a barrage of stress, thank God for Tua talking
> with Cade. And gosh darn it, if we didn't do some great work I work on georgebushtail.com.
> I'm around New Margaret, did some fine work. I had to keep pushing them to be productive and
> wasn't entirely successful, but we got some good stuff up and had some fun. And I'm feeling
> good about it.
>
> And we're running just one of the silliest banner ads I've ever seen. But I do want to see
> some naked George Bush action figures. very fine banner head graphics.

> June 15th, 2005, on Ogre Island doing some nature observation. I've been looking in the
> water and I found this. One fish that looks like it's in a mating, well, it's set up,
> they're four inch fish And I noticed that under branches, there were these fish swimming in
> a tight little pattern. They align themselves with the stick, sticks about an inch across
> maybe, and they quickly turn around, mainly staying under the stick, just maybe six inches
> long down to the sand And some of the sticks have, I assume, their nails staking out mating
> territory. At first I thought they looked perch-like, but then one spin flashing inside at
> me, and it's got sort of a silvery side. Oh, I see a dead one. I think I see a dead one.

> I'll have to get this and take a photograph. It seemed to me that they'd be very vulnerable

### `DW_A1034.mp3` — 2014-08-13 _(file date)_ — 3m46s

**Work:** `diary` · **Tags:** diary

> Diary just had the most delightful time snorkeling off Ogre. Got a 25 buck snorkel mask
> snorkel and split fin package and it's really sweet. I had to really learn how to get into
> the water with flippers on, and it's just gorgeous. I learned a lot. I did a big half circle
> out beyond the small point of the jay and through the weeds and the slimy weeds, the yellow,
> brown, it's like the kelp forests, the the coral of Martel Lake. That's life there. And I
> learned quite a few startling things occur. I was going through the lily pads, and a lot of
> it was going through lily pads and slimy weeds. But every so often there would be this moon
> loom out of the water, this golden moon, and it startled me. I thought, jeez, it's some fish
> or something. I steered
>
> away from it, the first one. And then one loomed up and, you know, I don't know what the
> visibility is. Three feet. So one loomed up right in front of me and I saw it was a lily
> pad, a young lily pad on the way to the surface. Small and yellow and they're all tilted at
> odd angles and got into an area where there's a couple. It's really beautiful. And another
> startling thing was, I was looking for my little Shiner nests, but I was out a little
> farther and all of a sudden, well not all of a sudden, gradually, I'm seeing nests dug out
> on the bottom. Some of them dug up weeds, and they're big. I mean, the nest that got away. I
> didn't have a yardstick with me, but I was a little paranoid. I sort of shied away from a
> couple of the big ones. I had this image of this big northern pike
>
> guarding its nest, taking a bite out of my face. And I was thinking, probably, it would It'd
> be cool to see a loon, of course, but I'm sure they can see me far, far away. But an otter
> would be insanely cool. Hey, I'll have to go nose around the beaver lodge with my snorkel
> on. God, scare the shit out of some beavers. Sounds like good times on Lake Martel.
>
